Let's design local optimizer more concretely.

Objective functions:
```
def conv_max_score(a): # a nonneg array sum maybe 1
    n = a.size
    m = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A*A, m)[:2*n-1]
    top = c.max() # c can small negative; if top<=0? invalid
    S = a.sum()
    return 2*n*top/(S*S), top, c
```
For speed, we can precompute for each n: m, idx for gradient.
Gradient for logsumexp:
```
def smooth_grad(a, c, top, T):
    weights = np.exp((c-top)/T)
    denom = weights.sum()
    # grad_i = 2/denom * sum_{j=0}^{n-1} w_{i+j} a_j
    wr = weights[::-1] # length 2n-1
    # Need full conv a (len n) and wr (len L) -> length n+L-1 = 3n-2. We only need indices L-1-i = 2n-2-i, i=0..n-1.
    ma = nextpow2(n+L-1)
    A = rfft(a, ma); W = rfft(wr, ma); h = irfft(A*W, ma)
    grad = 2 * h[L-1 - np.arange(n)] / denom
```
But computing extra FFT of weights each step. Weights length 2n-1, ma nextpow2(3n) ~4n, maybe more expensive. Alternative direct formula using c and a? grad_i = 2∑_k w_k a_{k-i}/denom. Could compute with sliding dot via FFT? Need efficient. Since n ~16k, direct O(n²) impossible. The extra conv may be heavy but still manageable. But we only need gradient approx; could restrict active weights (top K) and direct sum over active set? If T small, weights concentrated on active diagonal(s). For max objective, we can use active set of, say, all c > top - 0.01*top. That may be many (for flat, O(n)). But maybe can compute grad by direct for active lags: for each active k, grad += w_k * a[k-(n-1):?]. If active lags count R, O(nR) maybe if R ~n then O(n²). But with top count small maybe good. For general, FFT needed.

Can we compute gradient of max c only (single top lag) cheap: grad_i = a[k-i] valid. For k, vector = reversed segment of a. If top has few lags, direct. If near flat, many lags active. But if flat, gradient may be smooth; maybe use logsumexp but weight length 2n. The FFT once per objective anyway; gradient extra FFT. Could approximate gradient by smoothing weights with same FFT c? There is relationship: weights w_k, grad_i = ∑ w_{i+j} a_j. This is convolution of a with wr. We can compute via FFT. For each objective, we already have A=FFT(a) for c. We can compute W=FFT(wr,m_grad). m_grad nextpow2(3n-2). For n=16384, m=32768 for c, m_grad maybe 65536 (since 3n ~49152). rfft 65536 ~? 1ms. Two extra FFTs. 10000 steps maybe 10s. Fine.

But maybe use a simpler gradient: since objective max c, use subgradient at top K active lags. Active K can be chosen as count of c > top - eps*top, capped e.g. 64. Then gradient direct:
```
grad = zeros(n)
for k in active_k:
    # i range max(0,k-(n-1)) to min(n-1,k)
    # a_valid = a[i], paired? d/d a_i = 2 a_{k-i}; for active weight
    i = np.arange(max(0,k-n+1), min(n-1,k)+1)
    grad[i] += 2*w_k * a[k-i]
```
If active K small, cheap. In flat near target, maybe top lags not unique? But we can choose K=32 top lags (indices np.argpartition). That gives weighted sum of top diagonals. This is a smooth? Not differentiable but okay. It focuses on peak. Use weights proportional to c? Could do. This gradient avoids heavy second FFT. It is O(nK) ~16k*32=0.5M per step; 10k steps 5B operations if numpy loops over K with vector slices maybe heavy but manageable? 0.5M ~0.0005? times 10k=5s? Python loop 32 per step overhead. Could be okay. K maybe 16. But direct slice assignment with advanced indexing per lag; 32*10k=320k slices, okay.

Even simpler: Use top 1 lag subgradient: grad_i = a[k-i] valid. Then step down along reversed segment. This might reduce that diagonal. But if top moves, okay. This is like active set. For uniform, top central, grad constant; no movement. Need perturb. Starting asymmetric, top maybe offcenter; gradient will reduce. Could use top few lags with weights based on gaps.

- Compute c, top, F.
- Select active lags: indices of top B values (B=8 or 16), maybe all with c >= top - activity_tol*top (tol=0.02). But flat target may have many top values if F near 1.5? c_top ~0.75/n, tol 2% ~0.015/n; there could be hundreds. Cap to B=32 largest.

   for each k:

      # paired index j=k-i => from k-lo down to k-hi
      segment = a[lo:hi+1]
      grad[lo:hi+1] += w_k * segment[::-1]
   then normalize grad by sum w? Gradient with scale.
- Step: We want reduce c on active diagonals. For active lag k, derivative w.r.t a_i = a_{k-i}. To reduce c_k, move mass from positions where derivative large? Direction d_i ∝ - grad_i, with mean zero to preserve sum. But also nonneg.

```
step_ls:
    d = -grad + mean(grad)
    # rescale d to have L2 norm 1/sqrt(n)?
    d_norm = np.linalg.norm(d)/sqrt(n)
    if d_norm small: break
    alpha = 0.05 / d_norm? (values normalized)
    for m in range(12):
       a_try = a + alpha*d
       if min<0: clip? We can try sign: if d has large negative causing min negative, reduce.
       a_try = np.maximum(a_try,0)
       S=a_try.sum(); if S small continue; a_try/=S
       F_try=score(a_try)
       if F_try < F*(1-1e-10): accept; break
       alpha*=0.5
    if no accept, maybe stochastic perturb.
```
But step_scale: Since objective change O(alpha²|v|²), small. Could use alpha proportional to top? Let's scale d normalized to d∞ ~1/10. Values a ~1/n ~1e-4. d ~? grad ~a ~1e-4, normalized to d∞ 0.01?
```
target = 0.1 * a.max()
if target==0: target=1e-6
alpha = target / d∞
```
But d = -grad with mean zero already. The objective is homogeneous, so step relative.
- Backtracking up to maybe alpha*1.0 first. If all fail, do tiny random move.

Implementation of diagonal polarization per k:
```
def make_polarization_dir(a, active_k, alpha=1):
    d = zeros(n)
    for k in active_k:
        lo = max(0, k-(n-1)); hi = min(n-1,k)
        # pair i from lo to hi: j=k-i, include only i<j to avoid double. If i==j skip.
        i_min = lo; i_max = (hi)//2? Need i<j => i < k/2. Also i ≤ hi.
        imax = min(hi, (k-1)//2)
        for i in range(i_min, imax+1):
            j=k-i
            if i==j: continue
            if a[i] <= a[j]:
                amt = alpha * min(a[i], a[j], maybe scale)
            else: amt = alpha*min
            d[i] -= amt if a[i]<=a[j] else +amt? Let's write if a_i <= a_j: amt=min(a_i,a_j); d[i]-=amt; d[j]+=amt else amt=min; d[i]+=amt; d[j]-=amt.
```

Algorithm `polarize_improve(a, max_iters, initial_step)`:
```
a = normalize(a)
best = a.copy(); best_score=score(a)
active_count=8, maybe increase if plateau?
step_scale=0.25? # fraction of max
for it in range:
    c, top, score = get_c(a)
    if score < best_score: update
    if no improvement for ...: perturb
    # select active lags: top B by c; weights maybe powers of c/top or sorted
    # B could adapt: 1, then more when flat.
    d = zeros(n)
    for k in active:
       w = (c[k]/top)^2 maybe; or weight sorted descending
       lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)
       if lo<hi:
           # diff slice
           d[lo:hi+1] += w * (a[lo:hi+1] - a[hi:lo-1:-1])
    # d sum should ~0; remove mean to be safe
    d -= d.mean()
    norm = max(abs(d).max(), eps)
    # scale target: maybe target = 0.2 * max(a)  (since d same units a)
    alpha = target_frac / norm
    # Try alpha * factors [1,0.5,...]
    for factor in ...:
       a_try = a + alpha*factor*d
       if min(a_try)<0: maybe clip? But clipping may not keep direction; can allow clip to min(0,?) However to avoid huge negative, reduce.
       if a_try has negatives > small: reduce? We can just clip to 0 and renormalize; line search score. For smaller alpha, less negative.
       a_try = np.maximum(a_try,0)
       S = a_try.sum(); if S<=0 continue; a_try/=S
       sc = score(a_try)
       if sc < score - 1e-12: accept; break
    if no accept:
       # try smaller alpha or random move
```
But note d can have magnitude up to range a; normalized target 0.2*maxa means changes at most 20% of max pair. Good.

Local optimizer `local_search(a, max_time, max_iter, rng, verbose)`:

```
def local_search(a, stop_time, max_iter=20000, seed=1, tag=''):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    a = np.asarray(a, float).copy()
    a = normalize(a)
    best = a.copy(); best_score = score_only(a)
    current = a
    current_c, current_score, current_top = compute_c(score)
    dir_count = 1? maybe B=2
    step_frac=0.5? # maybe 0.5 of max diff
    no_improve=0
    noise=0.0
    perturb_interval=50
    for it in range(max_iter):
        if time.monotonic()>stop_time: break
        # add noise occasionally if no_improve > threshold
        if no_improve > 100:
            current = add_random_noise(current, noise)
            current_score, current_c = compute
            if score < best: update
            no_improve=0
            noise *= 1.5 (cap)
        # also every 200 maybe random diagonal
        # build direction
        d = build_direction(current, current_c, B, weights)
        if norm small: random perturbation
        # line search
        accepted=False
        alpha = scale from d
        for tries in range(8):
            trial = current + alpha*d
            if trial.min() < -1e-12: maybe trial=np.maximum(trial,0)
            else trial = trial.copy() (no clip)
            # If many negative, reduce alpha before score? We can clip anyway but may break line search; but try alpha halved until positive mostly. For small alpha, positive.
            if trial.min() < 0:
                # if min > -0.2*max maybe clip? else reduce
                frac = -min / max(dmin?); we can just clip to zero; score may still improve; if not reduce.
            trial = np.maximum(trial,0); S=trial.sum(); if S<... continue; trial/=S
            sc = score_only(trial)
            if sc < current_score*(1 - 1e-9) or sc < best_score*(1-...): accept, update best, current=trial
        if no accept:
            no_improve +=1
            # try tiny random move or step direction with very small alpha
            if no_improve % 10 ==0: do random pair or reset direction
        else: no_improve=0
```

Let's design direction function vectorized:
```
def make_direction(a, c, B=8, p=2, seed=None, rng=None, random_lags=False):
    n=a.size; L=2*n-1
    if rng is not None and random_lags:
        idx = rng.choice(L, size=B, replace=False)
    else:
        if B>=L: idx=np.arange(L)
        else: idx=np.argpartition(c, -B)[-B:]  # top B
    # weights
    w = c[idx]; w = np.maximum(w,0)
    # maybe downweight small: w = w**p; if sum zero use top1
    if w.sum()==0: w = np.ones_like(w)
    d=np.zeros(n)
    for k, wt in zip(idx, w):
        k=int(k); wt=float(wt)
        lo = 0 if k < n-1 else k-(n-1)
        hi = n-1 if k > n-1 else k
        if lo < hi:
            # If wt small skip
            d[lo:hi+1] += wt * (a[lo:hi+1] - a[hi:lo-1:-1])
    d -= wsum? no.
    d -= d.mean()
    return d, idx
```
Need handle `a[hi:lo-1:-1]` when lo=0: stop -1 gives up to 0. Good. When hi=lo: skip. For hi maybe n-1, start n-1 down to lo. length hi-lo+1, matches.
Weights normalization: We might want weights normalized to sum 1 or top weight 1. The direction magnitude scales; line search scales anyway. But using raw c weights (O(1/n)) gives small d; line search normalizes by d∞ to target, so relative weights only. Use normalized weights sum B or max 1. `w = w**p / w.max()`.
If B top selection includes c zero? Weights small. Good.

Line search function:
```
def line_search(a, d, current_score, best_score):
    if np.allclose(d,0): return None
    dmax = np.max(np.abs(d))
    if dmax ==0: return None
    # determine initial alpha such that expected change <= frac*range
    # Since d is dimension same as a, target change = 0.5 * np.mean(a?)? Use 0.5 * np.median(a nonzero)?? For values ~1/n, dmax maybe range ~2/n. If target=0.1*max(a). 
    scale = max(1e-300, a.max())
    alpha = 0.5 * scale / dmax  # 50% of max amplitude maybe. Could be too large; backtracking.
    # Also respect nonneg optional alpha_neg = min_{d<0} -a/d
    neg = d < 0
    if neg.any():
       alpha_neg = np.min(-a[neg] / -d[neg])? Actually d<0: need a + α d >=0 => α <= -a_i/d_i (d_i negative). alpha_neg = np.min(-a[neg]/d[neg])? d negative, -a/d = a/(-d). 
       if alpha_neg < alpha: alpha = 0.9*alpha_neg
    factors = [1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625]
    trial_best=None
    for factor in factors:
        aa=alpha*factor
        trial = a + aa*d
        if trial.min()<0:
           # if many negative? Clip; if min > -0.5*a.mean? Could accept clip? We can just clip.
           trial = np.maximum(trial,0)
        S=trial.sum()
        if S <= 1e-300: continue
        trial /= S
        sc=score_only(trial)
        if sc < current_score - 1e-12 and sc < current_score:
            return trial, sc, factor
    # if none, try very small
    aa = min(alpha, 1e-8*scale/dmax)
    ...
    return None
```

`compute_c_data(a)`:
```
def compute_convolution(a):
    n=a.size
    m=1
    need=2*n-1
    while m<need: m <<=1
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, m)
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    # eliminate tiny imaginary? irfft real.
    if c.size:
        top = float(np.max(c))
    else: top=0.0
    return c, top
```
Could optimize with `np.fft.rfftn`? no.
For score:
```
def score_from_c(c, a):
    S=a.sum()
    if S<=1e-12: return inf
    return 2*a.size*float(np.max(c))/(S*S)
```

```
def quick_score(a, need_c=False):
   S=a.sum()
   if S <= ...: return inf, None, 0
   n=a.size; m=...
   A=rfft(a,m); c=irfft(A*A,m)[:2n-1]
   top=c.max()
   score=2*n*top/(S*S)
   if need_c: return score,c,float(top)
   return score
```

1. `pow2_at_least(x)`:
```
def pow2_at_least(x):
    p=1
    while p<x: p <<=1
    return p
```

2. `normalize_positive(a)`:
```
def normalize_positive(a, eps=0.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    if eps>0: a = a + eps
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
       return np.ones_like(a)/a.size
    return a/s
```
We may not want add eps after each normalize; if a has zeros, fine. Use no eps.

3. `resample_sequence(src, n)`:
```
def resample_sequence(src, n):
    src = np.asarray(src, float).copy()
    src = np.clip(src,0,None)
    m=src.size
    if m==0: return np.ones(n)/n
    if m==n: return normalize_positive(src)
    if m==1: return np.full(n, src[0]/n)?? normalize
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    out=np.interp(x_new,x_old,src, left=src[0], right=src[-1])
    return normalize_positive(out)
```
Could memory heavy but fine.

7. `line_search_direction(a, d, current_score, current_sum?)`:
```
def try_step(a, d, score_current):
    dmax = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
    if dmax <= 1e-300: return None
    # initial step relative to a scale. Scale a maybe a.max()
    scale = float(a.max())
    if scale <= 0: return None
    alpha = 0.75 * scale / dmax
    # limit nonneg option
    neg = d < 0
    if neg.any():
       # α_max = min(-a/d) where d<0; if any a=0, α_max=0. We allow clip but not initial.
       vals = -a[neg] / d[neg]  # d negative, a>=0 => positive
       alpha_max = float(np.min(vals))
       if not np.isfinite(alpha_max) or alpha_max <=0:
           # use small fraction of scale if any zero; will clip
           alpha = min(alpha, 1e-6*scale/dmax)
       else:
           alpha = min(alpha, 0.9*alpha_max)
    # Now try factors [1,0.5,0.25,0.125,0.0625,0.03125]
    best_trial=None; best_sc=inf
    for factor in factors:
       aa=alpha*factor
       trial = a + aa*d
       # If negative, clip
       if trial.min() < 0:
           trial = np.maximum(trial, 0)
       s = float(trial.sum())
       if s <= 1e-12: continue
       trial = trial / s
       sc = quick_score(trial)
       if sc < current_score - 1e-12:
           return trial, sc
    # if none improved current, maybe try negative direction? We can in caller.
    return None
```
But if current_score is worse than best due exploration, a move improves current but not best; okay. If `a` has sum 1, normalize. Note quick_score recomputes sum=1 but okay.
Potential issue: If current_score inf? no.

8. `random_perturb(a, rng, sigma)`:
```
def random_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    n=a.size
    if sigma ==0: return a.copy()
    # multiplicative plus small additive anti-sym
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
    a2 = a * np.exp(noise)
    # optionally reverse mix
    if rng.random()<0.25:
        mix = rng.uniform(0.1,0.5)
        a2 += mix * (a - a[::-1]) * sigma  # sum may not zero? a - rev sum zero? sum a=sum rev, zero. good.
    a2 = np.maximum(a2,0)
    return normalize_positive(a2)
```
Need sum zero property? a - a[::-1] sum =0, yes. Good.

```
def local_search(a, time_limit, max_iter=100000, B=4, tag=''):
    a = normalize_positive(a)
    best = a.copy(); best_score = quick_score(a)
    current = a.copy()
    current_score = best_score
    c = compute_c_current? We'll compute in loop.
    no_improve=0
    sigma=0.03

    while it<max_iter and time.monotonic() < time.time()+time_limit:
       c, top = compute_convolution(current)
       # if current invalid, reset to best
       cur_score = 2*n*top/(current.sum()**2)
       if not finite: current=best; cur_score=best_score
       # deterministic direction
       d, idx = direction_top(current, c, Bcur)
       res = try_step(current, d, cur_score)
       if res is None:
           # try B2=1 or Bcur*2
           Btry = 1 if Bcur>1 else 8
           d, idx = direction_top(current, c, Btry)
           res = try_step(current, d, cur_score)
       if res is None:
           # random lags direction
           d, idx = direction_top(current, c, Bcur, random=True, rng=rng)
           res = try_step(current, d, cur_score)
       if res is not None:
           current, cur_score = res
           if cur_score < best_score - 1e-12:
               best = current.copy(); best_score=cur_score
               no_improve=0
           else:
               no_improve += 1 # improved current but not best? if exploring, maybe not count? If current was worse, should not reset? Might count high. Better if current score < best + 0.01, no_improve=0?
           # if significantly improved current, reduce sigma?

           no_improve += 1
           # If no improve, perturb
           if no_improve > 25:
               current = random_perturb(best if rng.random()<0.5 else current, rng, sigma)
               cur_score=quick_score(current)
               if cur_score < best_score:

               no_improve=0

               # try tiny fixed random direction?

           sigma = max(0.01, sigma*0.8)
       it +=1
       # maybe print every 200
    # after loop, if current score better? update best
    if quick_score(current) < best_score: best=current...
```

`pow2_at_least`:
```
def pow2_at_least(value):
    value = int(max(1, value))
    return 1 << (value - 1).bit_length()
```
If value=1 -> (0).bit_length()=0 =>1. Good.

`normalize_positive`:
```
def normalize_positive(a, floor=0.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0: return np.empty(0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    if floor > 0.0: a = a + floor
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / a.size
    return a / s
```

`resample_sequence`:
```
def resample_sequence(src, n):
    src = np.asarray(src, dtype=np.float64)
    src = np.clip(np.nan_to_num(src, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0), 0.0, None)
    n = int(n)
    if n <=0: return np.empty(0)
    if src.size == 0: return np.ones(n)/n
    if src.size == n: return normalize_positive(src)
    if src.size == 1:
        val = src[0]
        if val <=0: return np.ones(n)/n
        return np.full(n, val/n)
    # If interpolation with endpoint; use linear
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, src.size)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, src)
    return normalize_positive(out)
```
Could use `np.linspace` memory; okay.

`quick_score` and `quick_score_c`:
```
def quick_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s): return np.inf
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.inf
    m = pow2_at_least(2*n - 1)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, m)
    # c length m; first 2n-1
    if c.size >= 2*n - 1:
        top = float(np.max(c[:2*n - 1]))
    else:
        top = float(np.max(c))
    if top <= 0: top = 0.0
    return (2.0 * n * top) / (s * s)

def quick_score_with_c(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s) or a.size==0:
        return np.inf, np.array([]), 0.0
    n=a.size
    m=pow2_at_least(2*n-1)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,m)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,m)
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    top=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    score=(2.0*n*tope)/(s*s)
    return score, c, top
```
Could `np.max` on c with negative values; top positive if any positive. If all zero (invalid), score0 but impossible unless sum zero. Fine.

`direction_from_lags`:
```
def direction_from_lags(a, c, lags, weights):
    n = a.size
    d = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for pos, kk in enumerate(lags):
        k = int(kk)
        wt = float(weights[pos])
        if k < 0 or k >= 2*n - 1: continue
        if wt == 0.0: continue
        lo = max(0, k - (n - 1))
        hi = min(n - 1, k)
        if hi <= lo: continue
        # We can use buffer? 
        d[lo:hi+1] += wt * (a[lo:hi+1] - a[hi:lo-1:-1])
    return d
```
Need note: If `lo=0`, `a[hi:-1:-1]` okay? In Python, slice stop -1 means index 0? Yes: a[hi:-1:-1] includes 0. If hi=0? skip. If lo>0, `a[hi:lo-1:-1]` includes lo. Good.

`top_lags_weights`:
```
def select_lags(c, B, rng=None, random=False, power=2.0):
    L = c.size
    if L == 0: return np.empty(0,int), np.empty(0)
    B = int(min(B, L))
    if random:
        if B >= L:
            idx = np.arange(L)
        else:
            pool_size = min(L, max(B * 4, 16))
            # We want highest c in pool; use argpartition
            pool = np.argpartition(c, -pool_size)[-pool_size:]
            idx = rng.choice(pool, size=B, replace=False)
    else:
        if B >= L:
            idx = np.arange(L)
        else:
            # top B
            idx = np.argpartition(c, -B)[-B:]
    idx = np.asarray(idx, dtype=int)
    w = np.asarray(c[idx], dtype=float)
    w = np.where(w > 0, w, 0.0)
    # weight positive power? If all zero, top1 weight 1
    maxw = float(np.max(w)) if w.size else 0.0
    if maxw <= 0:
        # choose max index
        idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
        w = np.array([1.0])
        return idx, w
    w = w ** power
    # keep only nonzero? If power=0 etc
    maxw2 = float(np.max(w))
    if maxw2 > 0:
        w = w / maxw2
    # If some weights zero (c zero), could drop them? okay.
    return idx, w
```
If c has NaN? not. Power maybe 2. `w = w / maxw2` can keep zeros. Good.

`make_direction_list` maybe:
```
def make_primary_direction(a, c, B, rng, random=False, power=2.0):
    idx, w = select_lags(c, B, rng, random, power)
    d = direction_from_lags(a, c, idx, w)
    d -= d.mean()
    return d
```

`try_step`: Needs quick_score. To avoid normalizing input repeatedly? It does.
```
def try_step(a, d, current_score, alpha_multiplier=0.25, max_trials=6):
    if d.size != a.size or d.size ==0: return None
    dclean = np.nan_to_num(d, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    dmax = float(np.max(np.abs(dclean)))
    if dmax <= 1e-300: return None
    amax = float(np.max(a)) if a.size else 0.0
    if amax <= 0: return None
    alpha = alpha_multiplier * amax / dmax
    # nonneg limit optional
    neg = dclean < 0
    if neg.any():
        # ignore entries where a=0? gives 0
        ratios = -a[neg] / dclean[neg]
        ratios = ratios[ratios > 0]
        if ratios.size:
            alpha = min(alpha, 0.95 * float(np.min(ratios)))
    # factors tuple
    factors = (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625)
    # If alpha extremely small, also try 2.0
    for fac in factors:
        aa = alpha * fac
        if aa <=0: continue
        trial = a + aa * dclean
        # Clip negatives; maybe allow small negative clipped
        if trial.min() < 0.0:
            trial = np.maximum(trial, 0.0)
        s = float(trial.sum())
        if s <= 1e-15: continue
        trial = trial / s
        sc = quick_score(trial)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc + 1e-12 < current_score:
            return trial, sc
    # Try one larger step if not tried? maybe if alpha not limited by nonneg
    fac=2.0
    trial = a + 2.0*alpha*dclean
    ...
    return None
```
But if `alpha` was limited to near 0 due zeros, all factors tiny; okay. If `current_score` is bad. We can also return best among trials if slightly better? Not needed.
Potential issue: `trial.min()` on large array each factor; okay.

Could use local `quick_score` that assumes normalized? Trial normalized each time. good.

`random_perturb`:
```
def random_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return a.copy()
    if sigma <=0: return a.copy()
    # multiplicative lognormal
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
    a2 = a * np.exp(noise)
    r = rng.random()
    if r < 0.35:
        # additive antisymmetric noise
        h = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)
        h = h - h[::-1]
        # scale to add relative  sigma*median
        med = float(np.median(a[a>0])) if np.any(a>0) else 1.0/n
        a2 += sigma * med * h * 0.1
    # Another random block scaling? maybe
    if r < 0.2:
        width = max(1, n//50)
        start = int(rng.integers(0, n-width+1))
        factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.3)))
        a2[start:start+width] *= factor
    a2 = np.maximum(a2,0)
    return normalize_positive(a2)
```
Need avoid too complex; this okay. Could accidentally all zero if a had zeros and noise? If a has zeros, multiplied zeros remain zero; additive h can fill. Good.

`local_search`:

```
def make_start_list(n, rng, global_best=None, prior=None, num_starts=8):
    starts=[]
    # uniform
    starts.append(np.ones(n, dtype=float)/n)
    # global best resample
    if global_best is not None:
       starts.append(resample_sequence(global_best, n))
    # prior resample
    if prior is not None:
       starts.append(resample_sequence(prior, n))
    # analytic sine densities
    for alpha in (-1.0, 1.0, -0.75, 0.75, -0.5):
       starts.append(sine_density(n, alpha))
       if len(starts) >= num_starts: break? Need ensure random included.
    # random densities
    while len(starts) < num_starts:
       starts.append(random_density(n, rng))
    # deduplicate maybe by simple hash? not necessary
    return starts[:num_starts]
```

`sine_density(n, alpha)`:
```
def sine_density(n, alpha):
    if n<=0: return np.empty(0)
    t = np.arange(n, dtype=float)/float(n)  # or (i+0.5)/n
    f = 1.0 + alpha * np.sin(2.0*np.pi*t)
    f = np.clip(f,0,None)
    return normalize_positive(f)
```
For alpha ±1, zeros at some points. Good. Could include cosine variants:
`cos_density(n, alpha, phase)`. But random_density covers. Maybe analytic two-sine `1 + a sin(2πt) + b sin(4πt)`? Use random starts.

```
def random_density(n, rng):
    for attempt in range(5):
        t = np.arange(n, dtype=float)/float(n)
        K = int(rng.integers(3, 9)) # modes
        amps = rng.random(K)
        sall = amps.sum()
        if sall > 0:
           amp_scale = float(rng.uniform(0.4, 1.2)) / sall * 0.9? Want sum <=1.
           amps = amps * min(1.0, 0.9) *? Let's set amps = amps / max(1e-12, sall) * float(rng.uniform(0.5,1.0))
        base = 1.0
        f = np.full(n, base)
        for r in range(1,K+1):
            amp = float(amp? / r?) maybe to avoid high-freq negative; use amp * 1.0
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0, 2*np.pi))
            f += amp * np.cos(2*np.pi*r*t + phase)
        f = np.clip(f, 0, None)
        if f.sum() > 0:
            return normalize_positive(f)
    # fallback random nonnegative
    f = rng.random(n) + 1e-6
    return normalize_positive(f)
```

```
def run_stage(n, time_limit, n_starts, global_best, prior, extra, rng):
   if time_limit < 2: return
   starts = make_start_list(n, rng, global_best, prior, extra, n_starts)
   scored=[]
   for arr in starts:
      arr = normalize_positive(arr)
      if arr.size != n: arr = resample_sequence(arr,n)
      sc = quick_score(arr)
      if np.isfinite(sc): scored.append((sc, arr))
   if not scored: return
```

```
def update_pool(score, arr, pool, max_items=24):
    if not np.isfinite(score) or arr is None or arr.size==0: return
    arr = normalize_positive(arr).copy()
    pool.append((score, arr))
    if len(pool) > max_items:
        # find index with largest score
        worst_idx=0; worst_val=pool[0][0]
        for i in range(1,len(pool)):
            if pool[i][0] > worst_val:
                worst_val=pool[i][0]; worst_idx=i
        pool.pop(worst_idx)
```

```
def select_lags(c, B, max_lags=32, tol=0.02, rng=None, random=False, power=2.0):
   L=c.size
   if random... top pool
   top = c.max()
   if top <=0: ...
   thresh = top*(1.0-tol)
   idx_all = np.nonzero(c >= thresh)[0]
   if idx_all.size == 0: idx_all = np.array([np.argmax(c)])
   if idx_all.size > max_lags:
       # select top max_lags from all (global top max_lags)
       k = max_lags
       idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
       # If some of these not above? they are top so above.
   elif idx_all.size < max(1,B):
       # take top B but no more than max_lags
       k = min(L, max(B, idx_all.size))
       idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
   else:
       idx = idx_all
```

```
   arr=normalize(seed_arr)
   n=arr.size
   if n>512: return arr,score
   import linprog
   for _ in range(iterations):
      score,c,top=quick_score_with_c(arr)
      M = top * 1.0  # maybe with small increase? if top from arr.
      A = build A
      res=linprog(-ones, A_ub=A, b_ub=M, bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs')
      if res.x is None or not all finite: break
      b=normalize(res.x)

          arr = b
      else:
          # try M = top*0.98? Since f feasible at top, lower infeasible for f but might find b.

   return arr, quick_score(arr)
```

Let's design `lp_multi_step(initial_arr, iterations=3)`:
```
def lp_multi_step(initial_arr, iterations=3, max_n=256):
    arr=normalize_positive(initial_arr)
    n=arr.size
    if n<4 or n>max_n: return arr, quick_score(arr)
    try:
        from scipy.optimize import linprog
    except Exception:
        return arr, quick_score(arr)
    for it in range(iterations):
        score, c, top = quick_score_with_c(arr)
        M = float(top) * (1.0 + 1e-9) + 1e-15
        A = np.zeros((2*n-1,n))
        for i in range(n):
            val=arr[i]
            if val>0:
                A[i:i+n, i] = val
        cobj = -np.ones(n)
        bounds = [(0.0, None)]*n
        try:
           res = linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=M, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
        except Exception:
           break
        if res is None or res.x is None: continue
        cand = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=float)
        cand = np.clip(cand,0,None)
        cand = normalize_positive(cand)
        sc = quick_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < score - 1e-12:
             arr = cand
        else:
             # try slightly tighter M? Since b may improve if constraint tighter? But if M top, max sum may produce different. Try M*0.9?
             M2 = float(top)*0.98
             solve again; if sc2<score arr=cand2 else break
    return arr, quick_score(arr)
```

Now, let's think about the possibility that our local_search direction D_k may not be enough to reach target; maybe we can add a global "softmax gradient" direction using all c via FFT occasionally. Let's design efficient enough and include as `direction_logsumexp` if no_best > 200.

Compute weights from c: top=max(c); T = 0.05*top (or 0.1). weights = exp((c-top)/T); normalize sum to 1? Then gradient of logsumexp: grad_i = ∑ w_k a_{k-i}. We can compute via convolution with reversed weights. But memory for weights length L, FFT size 3n. For n=16k, m_grad=65536. We already have rfft of a length 32768 from previous c? Not stored. Could recompute. Cost 2 rffts + irfft ~3 FFTs, maybe 5ms. Occasional okay. Direction d = -grad - mean. Or use logsumexp D? The gradient is smooth and may move mass based on all diagonals. Implement:
```
def make_logsumexp_direction(a, c, power? temp_frac=0.05):
    L=c.size; n=a.size
    if L==0: return zeros
    top = float(np.max(c))
    if top <= 0: return zeros
    T = max(1e-300, temp_frac * top)
    # To avoid overflow, subtract top
    w = np.exp((c - top) / T)
    # c may negative, w small. sum maybe huge if many similar? If flat and T small, w~1 for many, sum~L, fine.
    w = w / w.sum()
    # Grad_i = sum_j a_j w_{i+j}. Use wr = w[::-1]
    m = pow2_at_least(3*n - 2)  # n + L -1
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    W = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], m)
    h = np.fft.irfft(A * W, m)
    # indices m? L-1-i where L=2n-1
    idx = np.arange(n)
    grad = h[L - 1 - idx]
    d = -grad
    d -= d.mean()
    return d
```
Check convolution length: a length n, wr length L=2n-1, full length n+L-1=3n-2. h index L-1-i = 2n-2-i. Good.
This direction is negative gradient of logsumexp? Since ∂/∂a_i ∑_k c_k = 2 a_{k-i}. With weights normalized, grad (without 2) = ∑ w a_{k-i}. Negative reduces weighted c. Good. It may be more global. Add in local_search after many fails.
```
if res is None and no_best > 40:
    d = make_logsumexp_direction(current, c, temp_frac=0.1)
    res=try_step(current,d,current_score)
```
But temp_frac: If top=1e-3, T=1e-4, many weights; if flat, w equal, gradient ≈ mean of reversed a, maybe constant? Let's see if c flat, w flat length 2n-1. grad_i = ∑_{j} a_j (for i+j valid) not constant near edges; interior sum=1, edges <1. It will move mass away from edges? Good. Could help flatten.
But if T too small, only active. Use 0.05 to 0.2. We can call with 0.1.
This extra code top-level. Good.

`make_start_list` details no lambda:
```
def make_start_list(n, rng, global_best=None, prior=None, extra=None, num_starts=8):
    n=int(n)
    if n<=0: return []
    starts=[]
    starts.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)/float(n))
    if global_best is not None:
        starts.append(resample_sequence(global_best, n))
        # mixed with reverse
        gb = np.asarray(global_best, dtype=np.float64)
        gb = resample_sequence(gb, n)
        p = float(rng.uniform(0.15, 0.85))
        mixed = p*gb + (1-p)*gb[::-1]
        starts.append(normalize_positive(mixed))
    if prior is not None:
        starts.append(resample_sequence(prior, n))
    if extra is not None:
        count=0
        for seq in extra:
            if count>=2: break
            if seq is not None and seq.size>0:
                starts.append(resample_sequence(seq, n)); count+=1
    # analytic sines
    for alpha in (-1.0, 1.0, -0.75, 0.75, -0.5, 0.5):
        if len(starts) >= num_starts: break
        starts.append(sine_density(n, alpha))
    # random densities until num_starts
    while len(starts) < num_starts:
        starts.append(random_density(n, rng))
    # optionally random nonnegative spikes
    if len(starts) < num_starts + 2:
        f = rng.random(n) + 1e-6
        starts.append(normalize_positive(f))
    return starts[:num_starts]
```
If num_starts=1, only uniform. But we call with >=3. Need if global_best None but prior etc, still enough.
Potential duplicate penalty? no.

`random_density`: Ensure if f sum zero, fallback. Use rng available.
```
def random_density(n, rng):
    n=int(n)
    if n<=0: return np.empty(0)
    for attempt in range(6):
        t = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)/float(n)
        K = int(rng.integers(3, 9))
        amps = rng.random(K)
        s = float(amps.sum())
        if s > 0:
            scale = float(rng.uniform(0.35, 1.0))
            amps = amps / s * scale
        else:
            amps = np.zeros(K)
        f = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for r in range(1, K+1):
            if amps[r-1] == 0: continue
            phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0*np.pi))
            f += amps[r-1] * np.cos(2.0*np.pi*r*t + phase)
        f = np.clip(f, 0.0, None)
        if f.sum() > 0:
            return normalize_positive(f)
    f = rng.random(n) + 1e-6
    return normalize_positive(f)
```
Could use sine instead of cos; phase covers.

`select_lags`: Use no lambdas.
```
def select_lags(c, B, max_lags=32, tol=0.0, rng=None, random=False, power=2.0):
    L = int(c.size)
    if L <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=int), np.empty(0, dtype=float)
    B = int(max(1, min(B, L)))
    top = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(top) or top <= 0:
        idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))]) if c.size else np.empty(0)
        return idx, np.ones_like(idx, dtype=float)
    if random and rng is not None:
        pool_size = min(L, max(B*4, 16))
        if pool_size >= L:
            pool = np.arange(L)
        else:
            pool = np.argpartition(c, -pool_size)[-pool_size:]
        idx = rng.choice(pool, size=B, replace=False)
    else:
        if tol > 0:
            threshold = top * (1.0 - tol)
            above = np.nonzero(c >= threshold)[0]
            if above.size == 0:
                above = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
            if above.size > max_lags:
                # pick top max_lags globally
                k = min(L, max_lags)
                idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
            elif above.size < B:
                k = min(L, B)
                idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
            else:
                idx = above
        else:
            k = min(L, B)
            idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
    idx = np.asarray(idx, dtype=int)
    w = np.asarray(c[idx], dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.where(w > 0, w, 0.0)
    if w.size == 0:
        w = np.ones(idx.size)
    else:
        maxw = float(np.max(w))
        if maxw <= 0 or not np.isfinite(maxw):
             w = np.ones(idx.size)
        else:
             w = w ** power
             maxw2 = float(np.max(w))
             if maxw2 > 0:
                 w = w / maxw2
             else:
                 w = np.ones(idx.size)
    return idx, w
```
Need if c has NaN? not. `np.argmax(c)` if c finite. fine.
We can call with tol=0.02. Then max_lags 32. If above many, top 32. If above small, top B. Good.
For random, not using tol; maybe okay. Could random choose from top pool.

`make_primary_direction` calls select_lags and direction.
```
def make_primary_direction(a,c,B,tol=0.02,rng=None,random=False,power=2.0):
    idx,w = select_lags(c,B,max_lags=32,tol=tol,rng=rng,random=random,power=power)
    d = direction_from_lags(a,c,idx,w)
    d -= d.mean()
    return d
```

`direction_from_lags` if idx empty returns zeros.

`make_logsumexp_direction`:
```
def make_logsumexp_direction(a,c,temp_frac=0.08):
    n=a.size; L=c.size
    d=np.zeros(n)
    if n==0 or L==0: return d
    top=float(np.max(c))
    if top <=0 or not np.isfinite(top): return d
    T=top*temp_frac
    if T <= 1e-300: T=1e-300
    # Ensure c clipped nonnegative? Use c_raw
    vals = c - top
    # if T small and vals negative large, underflow.
    with np.errstate(over='ignore', under='ignore'):
        w = np.exp(vals / T)
    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
        # fallback uniform top?
        # top indices
        idx = np.nonzero(c >= top*0.999)[0]
        w = np.zeros(L); if idx.size: w[idx]=1
        s=w.sum(); if s<=0: return d
    w = w / s
    m = pow2_at_least(3*n - 2)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    W = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], m)
    h = np.fft.irfft(A * W, m)
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=int)
    grad = h[L - 1 - idx]
    if grad.size != n:
        grad = grad[:n]
    d = -grad
    d -= d.mean()
    return d
```
`np.errstate` okay. Need avoid overflow if vals/T >709; but vals≤0, so no overflow positive. Underflow fine. Good.

`local_search` details time. Use `stop = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(time_limit))`. If time_limit <=0 return start, quick_score.
```
def local_search(start, time_limit, rng, base_B=8, max_iter=1000000):
    start=normalize_positive(start)
    best=start.copy()
    best_score=quick_score(best)
    current=start.copy()
    current_score=best_score
    B=base_B
    sigma=0.02
    no_best=0
    it=0
    stop=time.monotonic()+max(0.0,float(time_limit))
    while it<max_iter and time.monotonic()<stop:
       if current.sum() <= 1e-15:
          current=best.copy(); current_score=best_score
       score, c, top = quick_score_with_c(current)
       if not np.isfinite(score):
          current=best.copy(); current_score=best_score; continue
       if score < current_score - 1e-12: current_score=score
       if current_score < best_score - 1e-12:
          best=current.copy(); best_score=current_score; no_best=0
       else:
          no_best += 1
       # directions
       d = make_primary_direction(current, c, B, tol=0.02, rng=None, random=False, power=2.0)
       res = try_step(current, d, current_score)
       if res is None:
           if B > 8:
              B_alt = 8
           else:
              B_alt = 1
           d = make_primary_direction(current, c, B_alt, tol=0.0, rng=None, random=False, power=2.0)
           res = try_step(current, d, current_score)
       if res is None and no_best > 10:
           d = make_primary_direction(current, c, max(B,8), tol=0.0, rng=rng, random=True, power=2.0)
           res = try_step(current, d, current_score)
       if res is None and no_best > 40 and (no_best % 10 == 0):
           d = make_logsumexp_direction(current, c, 0.08)
           res = try_step(current, d, current_score)
       if res is not None:
           current, current_score = res
           if current_score < best_score - 1e-12:
               best=current.copy(); best_score=current_score; no_best=0
           else:
               no_best += 1
       else:
           no_best += 1
           if no_best > 60:
               if rng.random() < 0.75:
                   current = random_perturb(best, rng, sigma)
               else:
                   current = random_perturb(current, rng, sigma)
               current_score = quick_score(current)
               if current_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                   best=current.copy(); best_score=current_score; no_best=0
               else:
                   no_best=0
               sigma = min(0.8, sigma*1.4)
       # adapt B
       if no_best > 120:
           B = min(32, max(2, B*2))
       elif no_best < 5:
           B = max(4, B//2)
       it += 1
       # optionally if time > stop break
    # final check
    sc = quick_score(current)
    if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
       best=current.copy(); best_score=sc
    return best, best_score
```

`lp_multi_step` uses `quick_score_with_c` and `linprog`. Need maybe not use if time limited? We'll call in run_stage only small n.

Implement LP A construction memory efficient? Dense okay.
```
def lp_multi_step(initial_arr, iterations=2, max_n=256):
    arr = normalize_positive(initial_arr)
    n=arr.size
    if n < 4 or n > max_n: return arr, quick_score(arr)
    try:
        from scipy.optimize import linprog
    except Exception:
        return arr, quick_score(arr)
    score_best = quick_score(arr)
    for _ in range(iterations):
        score, c, top = quick_score_with_c(arr)
        if not np.isfinite(score): break
        M = float(top) * (1.0 + 1e-9) + 1e-12
        A = np.zeros((2*n-1, n), dtype=np.float64)
        for j in range(n):
            A[j:j+n, j] = arr
        cobj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
        improved=False
        for Mval in (M, max(1e-15, float(top)*0.98)):
            try:
                res = linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=Mval, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
            except Exception:
                res = None
            if res is None or not getattr(res, 'success', False) or res.x is None:
                continue
            cand = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, None)
            cand = normalize_positive(cand)
            sc = quick_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < score - 1e-10:
                arr = cand
                improved=True
                break
        if not improved:
            break
    return arr, quick_score(arr)
```
Note `b_ub` changed for second solve; A same. `linprog` may take time. If `res.success` false but x maybe; skip.
Could `A[j:j+n, j] = arr` for j=n-1 gives slice n-1:2n-1 length n. okay.
`quick_score` at end may be expensive. ok.

Sorting starts: Without lambda, use order array.
```
def sort_scored(scored):
    if len(scored)==0: return scored
    scores = np.empty(len(scored), dtype=float)
    for i in range(len(scored)): scores[i]=scored[i][0]
    order = np.argsort(scores)
    out=[]
    for i in order: out.append(scored[int(i)])
    return out
```
Top-level helper.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=1234, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    budget=float(budget_s)
    reserve=120.0
    deadline=t0 + max(120.0, budget - reserve)

    pool=[]

    prior=get_prior_sequence()

    stages = [
       (256, 0.015, 12),
       (512, 0.025, 10),
       (1024, 0.05, 9),
       (2048, 0.08, 8),
       (4096, 0.12, 7),
       (8192, 0.15, 6),
       (16384, 0.18, 5),
       (32768, 0.0, 4) # last remaining
    ]
    for idx_stage in range(len(stages)):
       n, frac, n_starts = stages[idx_stage]
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30: break

           stage_time = search_time * frac
           if time.monotonic() + stage_time > deadline - 30:
               stage_time = max(1.0, deadline - 30 - time.monotonic())

           stage_time = max(0.0, deadline - 30 - time.monotonic())
```

```
       count +=1
```

`exact_evaluate`:
```
def exact_evaluate(arr):
    fn = globals().get('evaluate_sequence', None)
    if fn is None: return np.inf
    try:
        a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
        a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
        seq = [float(x) for x in a]
        if not seq: return np.inf
        val = fn(seq)
        return float(val)
    except Exception:
        return np.inf
```
Good.

```
def quick_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if a.size==0 or s<=1e-15: return np.inf
    n=a.size
    m=pow2_at_least(2*n-1)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,m)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A,m)
    top=float(np.max(c[:2*n-1])) if c.size >= 2*n-1 else float(np.max(c))
    if top <0: top=0.0 # but max negative? If all zero, 0.
    return (2.0*n*top)/(s*s)
```

```
def sort_scored(items):
    if not items: return []
    scores = np.empty(len(items), dtype=np.float64)
    for i,item in enumerate(items): scores[i]=item[0]
    order = np.argsort(scores)
    out=[]
    for i in order: out.append(items[int(i)])
    return out
```

`update_pool`: If `arr` is not finite? It normalizes; if fails returns uniform. But if arr contains NaN, normalize uses nan_to_num. Good.
```
def update_pool(score, arr, pool, max_items=24):
    try:
       sc=float(score)
    except Exception:
       return
    if not np.isfinite(sc) or arr is None: return
    a=np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return
    a=normalize_positive(a)
    pool.append((sc, a.copy()))
    if len(pool)>max_items:
       worst=0; worst_val=pool[0][0]
       for i in range(1,len(pool)):
          if pool[i][0] > worst_val:
             worst_val=pool[i][0]; worst=i
       pool.pop(worst)
```

```
    if arr.size==0: return None
    arr=np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr=np.clip(arr,0,None)
    if float(arr.sum()) <=1e-15: return None
    return normalize_positive(arr)
```

```python
"""
Multi-resolution active-diagonal polarization search.

The search minimizes
    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / sum(a)^2
over nonnegative sequences.  The main local move is to pick the largest
convolution anti-diagonals and apply the pair-sum-preserving direction

    d_i += a_i - a_{k-i}

for those diagonals k.  This is the projected negative gradient direction for
the active quadratic constraints.  Random perturbations, occasional log-sum-exp
gradients, and an optional small-n LP diversifier help escape local basins.

Several lengths are searched in increasing order, and the best internal
candidate is verified with the supplied evaluate_sequence() when time remains.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def pow2_at_least(value):
    value = int(max(1, value))
    return 1 << (value - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_positive(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    return a / s


def resample_sequence(src, n):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    src = np.asarray(src, dtype=np.float64)
    if src.size == 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(src)):
        src = np.nan_to_num(src, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    src = np.clip(src, 0.0, None)
    if src.size == 1:
        v = float(src[0])
        if v <= 0.0:
            return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        return np.full(n, v / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    if src.size == n:
        return normalize_positive(src)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, src.size)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, src)
    return normalize_positive(out)


def quick_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return np.inf
    n = a.size
    m = pow2_at_least(2 * n - 1)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, m)
    need = 2 * n - 1
    if c.size >= need:
        top = float(np.max(c[:need]))
    else:
        top = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(top) or top <= 0.0:
        top = 0.0
    return (2.0 * float(n) * top) / (s * s)


def quick_score_with_c(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.inf, np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64), 0.0
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return np.inf, np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64), 0.0
    n = a.size
    m = pow2_at_least(2 * n - 1)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, m)
    need = 2 * n - 1
    if c.size >= need:
        c = c[:need]
    else:
        c = c
    top = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(top) or top <= 0.0:
        top = 0.0
    score = (2.0 * float(n) * top) / (s * s)
    return score, c, top


def sine_density(n, alpha):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    t = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    f = 1.0 + float(alpha) * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * t)
    f = np.clip(f, 0.0, None)
    return normalize_positive(f)


def random_density(n, rng):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    for _ in range(6):
        t = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
        k = int(rng.integers(3, 9))
        amps = rng.random(k)
        s = float(amps.sum())
        scale = float(rng.uniform(0.35, 1.0))
        if s > 0.0:
            amps = amps / s * scale
        else:
            amps = np.zeros(k, dtype=np.float64)
        f = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for r in range(1, k + 1):
            amp = float(amps[r - 1])
            if amp == 0.0:
                continue
            phi = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
            f += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * r * t + phi)
        f = np.clip(f, 0.0, None)
        if float(f.sum()) > 0.0:
            return normalize_positive(f)
    f = rng.random(n) + 1e-6
    return normalize_positive(f)


def make_start_list(n, rng, global_best=None, prior=None, extra=None, num_starts=8):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return []
    starts = []
    starts.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n))

    if global_best is not None:
        gb = resample_sequence(global_best, n)
        starts.append(gb)
        p = float(rng.uniform(0.15, 0.85))
        mixed = p * gb + (1.0 - p) * gb[::-1]
        starts.append(normalize_positive(mixed))

    if prior is not None:
        starts.append(resample_sequence(prior, n))

    if extra is not None:
        cnt = 0
        for seq in extra:
            if cnt >= 2:
                break
            if seq is not None:
                arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
                if arr.size > 0:
                    starts.append(resample_sequence(arr, n))
                    cnt += 1

    alphas = (-1.0, 1.0, -0.75, 0.75, -0.5, 0.5)
    for alpha in alphas:
        if len(starts) >= num_starts:
            break
        starts.append(sine_density(n, alpha))

    while len(starts) < num_starts:
        starts.append(random_density(n, rng))

    # A few noisy fully supported starts.
    for _ in range(2):
        if len(starts) >= num_starts + 2:
            break
        starts.append(normalize_positive(rng.random(n) + 1e-6))

    return starts[:max(1, int(num_starts))]


def sort_scored(items):
    if not items:
        return []
    scores = np.empty(len(items), dtype=np.float64)
    for i, item in enumerate(items):
        scores[i] = float(item[0])
    order = np.argsort(scores)
    out = []
    for i in order:
        out.append(items[int(i)])
    return out


def update_pool(score, arr, pool, max_items=24):
    try:
        sc = float(score)
    except Exception:
        return
    if not np.isfinite(sc) or arr is None:
        return
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return
    a = normalize_positive(a)
    pool.append((sc, a.copy()))
    if len(pool) > max_items:
        worst = 0
        worst_val = float(pool[0][0])
        for i in range(1, len(pool)):
            v = float(pool[i][0])
            if v > worst_val:
                worst_val = v
                worst = i
        pool.pop(worst)


def select_lags(c, B, max_lags=32, tol=0.0, rng=None, random=False, power=2.0):
    L = int(c.size)
    if L <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=int), np.empty(0, dtype=float)
    B = int(max(1, min(B, L)))
    top = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(top) or top <= 0.0:
        idx = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)
        return idx, np.ones(idx.size, dtype=np.float64)

    if random and rng is not None:
        pool_size = min(L, max(B * 4, 16))
        if pool_size >= L:
            pool = np.arange(L, dtype=int)
        else:
            pool = np.argpartition(c, -pool_size)[-pool_size:]
        if B >= int(pool.size):
            idx = pool.copy()
        else:
            idx = rng.choice(pool, size=B, replace=False)
    else:
        if tol > 0.0:
            threshold = top * (1.0 - tol)
            above = np.nonzero(c >= threshold)[0]
            if above.size == 0:
                above = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=int)
            if int(above.size) > max_lags:
                k = min(L, max_lags)
                if k >= L:
                    idx = np.arange(L, dtype=int)
                else:
                    idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
            elif int(above.size) < B:
                k = min(L, B)
                if k >= L:
                    idx = np.arange(L, dtype=int)
                else:
                    idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]
            else:
                idx = above
        else:
            k = min(L, B)
            if k >= L:
                idx = np.arange(L, dtype=int)
            else:
                idx = np.argpartition(c, -k)[-k:]

    idx = np.asarray(idx, dtype=int)
    w = np.asarray(c[idx], dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.where(w > 0.0, w, 0.0)
    if w.size == 0:
        return idx, np.ones(idx.size, dtype=np.float64)

    maxw = float(np.max(w))
    if not np.isfinite(maxw) or maxw <= 0.0:
        return idx, np.ones(idx.size, dtype=np.float64)

    w = w ** float(power)
    maxw2 = float(np.max(w))
    if maxw2 > 0.0 and np.isfinite(maxw2):
        w = w / maxw2
    else:
        w = np.ones(idx.size, dtype=np.float64)

    return idx, w


def direction_from_lags(a, lags, weights, rng=None, random_sign=False):
    n = int(a.size)
    d = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 0 or lags.size == 0:
        return d
    for col, kk in enumerate(lags):
        k = int(kk)
        if col < weights.size:
            wt = float(weights[col])
        else:
            wt = 0.0
        if wt == 0.0 or k < 0 or k >= 2 * n - 1:
            continue
        if random_sign and rng is not None and rng.random() < 0.25:
            wt = -wt
        lo = max(0, k - (n - 1))
        hi = min(n - 1, k)
        if hi <= lo:
            continue
        d[lo:hi + 1] += wt * (a[lo:hi + 1] - a[hi:lo - 1:-1])
    return d


def make_primary_direction(a, c, B, tol=0.02, rng=None, random=False, power=2.0,
                           random_sign=False):
    idx, w = select_lags(c, B, max_lags=32, tol=tol, rng=rng, random=random, power=power)
    d = direction_from_lags(a, idx, w, rng=rng, random_sign=random_sign)
    if d.size:
        d -= d.mean()
    return d


def make_logsumexp_direction(a, c, temp_frac=0.08):
    n = int(a.size)
    L = int(c.size)
    d = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 0 or L == 0:
        return d
    top = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(top) or top <= 0.0:
        return d
    t = max(1e-300, float(temp_frac) * top)
    with np.errstate(over="ignore", under="ignore"):
        w = np.exp((c - top) / t)
    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        nz = np.nonzero(c >= top * 0.999)[0]
        if nz.size:
            w[nz] = 1.0
        s = float(np.sum(w))
        if s <= 0.0:
            return d
    w = w / s

    m = pow2_at_least(3 * n - 2)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, m)
    W = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], m)
    h = np.fft.irfft(A * W, m)
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=int)
    grad = h[L - 1 - idx]
    if grad.size != n:
        grad = grad[:n]
    d = -grad
    if d.size:
        d -= d.mean()
    return d


def try_step(a, d, current_score, alpha_multiplier=0.18, max_trials=6):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    d = np.asarray(d, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0 or d.size != a.size:
        return None
    if not np.isfinite(current_score):
        return None
    d = np.nan_to_num(d, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    dmax = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
    if not np.isfinite(dmax) or dmax <= 1e-300:
        return None
    amax = float(np.max(a))
    if not np.isfinite(amax) or amax <= 0.0:
        return None

    alpha = float(alpha_multiplier) * amax / dmax

    neg = d < 0.0
    if neg.any():
        ratios = -a[neg] / d[neg]
        ratios = ratios[ratios > 0.0]
        if ratios.size:
            mr = float(np.min(ratios))
            if mr > 0.0:
                alpha = min(alpha, 0.95 * mr)
            else:
                alpha = min(alpha, 1e-3 * amax / dmax)

    factors = (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.125, 0.0625, 2.0)
    for fac in factors:
        aa = alpha * float(fac)
        if aa <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(aa):
            continue
        trial = a + aa * d
        if trial.min() < 0.0:
            trial = np.maximum(trial, 0.0)
        s = float(trial.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
            continue
        trial = trial / s
        sc = quick_score(trial)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc + 1e-11 < current_score:
            return trial, sc
    return None


def random_perturb(a, rng, sigma):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a.copy()
    if sigma <= 0.0:
        return a.copy()

    noise = rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), n)
    a2 = a * np.exp(noise)

    r = float(rng.random())
    if r < 0.35:
        h = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)
        h = h - h[::-1]
        pos = a > 0.0
        if pos.any():
            med = float(np.median(a[pos]))
        else:
            med = 1.0 / float(n)
        a2 += 0.2 * float(sigma) * med * h

    if r < 0.20:
        width = max(1, n // 50)
        if width < n:
            start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
            factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.25)))
            a2[start:start + width] *= factor

    return normalize_positive(a2)


def local_search(start, time_limit, rng, base_B=8, max_iter=1000000):
    start = normalize_positive(start)
    best = start.copy()
    best_score = quick_score(best)
    current = start.copy()
    current_score = best_score

    if time_limit is None:
        time_limit = 0.0
    stop = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(time_limit))

    B = int(base_B)
    sigma = 0.02
    no_best = 0
    it = 0

    while it < max_iter and time.monotonic() < stop:
        if float(current.sum()) <= 1e-15:
            current = best.copy()
            current_score = best_score

        score, c, _ = quick_score_with_c(current)
        if not np.isfinite(score):
            current = best.copy()
            current_score = best_score
            it += 1
            continue

        current_score = float(score)
        improved_best = False
        if current_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = current.copy()
            best_score = current_score
            improved_best = True

        moved = False

        d = make_primary_direction(current, c, B, tol=0.02, rng=None, random=False,
                                   power=2.0, random_sign=False)
        res = try_step(current, d, current_score)
        if res is not None:
            current, current_score = res
            moved = True
            if current_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best = current.copy()
                best_score = current_score
                improved_best = True

        if not moved:
            b_alt = 1 if B > 1 else 8
            d = make_primary_direction(current, c, b_alt, tol=0.0, rng=None, random=False,
                                       power=2.0, random_sign=False)
            res = try_step(current, d, current_score)
            if res is not None:
                current, current_score = res
                moved = True
                if current_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best = current.copy()
                    best_score = current_score
                    improved_best = True

        if not moved and no_best > 10:
            d = make_primary_direction(current, c, max(B, 8), tol=0.0, rng=rng, random=True,
                                       power=2.0, random_sign=True)
            res = try_step(current, d, current_score)
            if res is not None:
                current, current_score = res
                moved = True
                if current_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best = current.copy()
                    best_score = current_score
                    improved_best = True

        if not moved and no_best > 40 and (no_best % 5 == 0):
            d = make_logsumexp_direction(current, c, 0.08)
            res = try_step(current, d, current_score)
            if res is not None:
                current, current_score = res
                moved = True
                if current_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best = current.copy()
                    best_score = current_score
                    improved_best = True

        if no_best > 60:
            if rng.random() < 0.75:
                current = random_perturb(best, rng, sigma)
            else:
                current = random_perturb(current, rng, sigma)
            current_score = quick_score(current)
            if np.isfinite(current_score) and current_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best = current.copy()
                best_score = current_score
                improved_best = True
            no_best = 0
            sigma = min(0.8, sigma * 1.4)

        if improved_best:
            no_best = 0
        else:
            no_best += 1

        if no_best > 120:
            B = min(32, max(2, B * 2))
        elif no_best < 5:
            B = max(4, B // 2)

        it += 1

    sc = quick_score(current)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
        best = current.copy()
        best_score = sc

    return best, best_score


def get_prior_sequence():
    g = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if g is None:
        return None
    arr = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, None)
    if float(arr.sum()) <= 1e-15:
        return None
    return normalize_positive(arr)


def lp_multi_step(initial_arr, iterations=2, max_n=256):
    arr = normalize_positive(initial_arr)
    n = int(arr.size)
    if n < 4 or n > int(max_n):
        return arr, quick_score(arr)

    try:
        from scipy.optimize import linprog
    except Exception:
        return arr, quick_score(arr)

    for _ in range(max(0, int(iterations))):
        score, _c, top = quick_score_with_c(arr)
        if not np.isfinite(score):
            break
        M = float(top) * (1.0 + 1e-9) + 1e-12
        A = np.zeros((2 * n - 1, n), dtype=np.float64)
        for j in range(n):
            A[j:j + n, j] = arr
        cobj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
        improved = False

        for Mval in (M, max(1e-15, float(top) * 0.98)):
            b_ub = np.full(2 * n - 1, float(Mval), dtype=np.float64)
            try:
                res = linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method="highs")
            except Exception:
                res = None
            if res is None or not getattr(res, "success", False) or res.x is None:
                continue
            cand = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, None)
            cand = normalize_positive(cand)
            sc = quick_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < score - 1e-10:
                arr = cand
                improved = True
                break
        if not improved:
            break

    return arr, quick_score(arr)


def run_stage(n, stage_time, n_starts, global_best, prior, extra, rng):
    n = int(n)
    stage_time = float(stage_time)
    if n <= 0 or stage_time < 1.0:
        return []

    starts = make_start_list(n, rng, global_best=global_best, prior=prior,
                             extra=extra, num_starts=max(1, int(n_starts)))
    scored = []
    for arr in starts:
        arr = normalize_positive(arr)
        if arr.size != n:
            arr = resample_sequence(arr, n)
        if arr.size != n or arr.size == 0:
            continue
        sc = quick_score(arr)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((sc, arr.copy()))

    if not scored:
        scored = [(np.inf, np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n))]

    scored = sort_scored(scored)

    # Optional small LP diversification.
    if n <= 256 and stage_time > 12.0:
        for i in range(min(2, len(scored))):
            sc, arr = scored[i]
            try:
                arr2, sc2 = lp_multi_step(arr, iterations=2, max_n=256)
            except Exception:
                arr2, sc2 = arr, sc
            if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < sc:
                scored[i] = (sc2, arr2.copy())
        scored = sort_scored(scored)

    weights = []
    total_w = 0.0
    for i in range(len(scored)):
        w = 2.0 if i < 2 else 1.0
        weights.append(float(w))
        total_w += float(w)

    results = []
    stage_end = time.monotonic() + stage_time
    for i in range(len(scored)):
        remaining = stage_end - time.monotonic()
        if remaining < 2.0:
            break
        per = stage_time * weights[i] / max(1e-30, total_w)
        per = min(per, remaining)
        if i >= 2 and per < 0.5:
            continue
        sc, arr = scored[i]
        try:
            arr2, sc2 = local_search(arr, per, rng, base_B=8)
        except Exception:
            arr2, sc2 = arr, sc
        if np.isfinite(sc2):
            results.append((sc2, arr2.copy()))

    return results


def exact_evaluate(arr):
    fn = globals().get("evaluate_sequence", None)
    if fn is None:
        return np.inf
    try:
        a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
        if a.size == 0:
            return np.inf
        a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
        seq = [float(x) for x in a]
        val = fn(seq)
        return float(val)
    except Exception:
        return np.inf


def propose_candidate(seed=1234, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    if budget_s is None:
        if "budget_s" in kwargs:
            budget_s = kwargs["budget_s"]
        elif "time_limit" in kwargs:
            budget_s = kwargs["time_limit"]
        else:
            budget_s = 2400.0

    total_time = float(budget_s)
    if not np.isfinite(total_time) or total_time <= 0.0:
        total_time = 2400.0

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    reserve = min(160.0, max(40.0, 0.06 * total_time))
    search_time = max(60.0, total_time - reserve)
    search_deadline = t0 + search_time
    usable_deadline = search_deadline - 20.0

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    pool = []

    best_arr = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64) / 1024.0
    best_score = quick_score(best_arr)
    update_pool(best_score, best_arr, pool, max_items=24)

    prior = get_prior_sequence()

    # (length, fraction_of_search_time, number_of_starts)
    stages = [
        (256,   0.015, 12),
        (512,   0.025, 10),
        (1024,  0.050, 9),
        (2048,  0.080, 8),
        (4096,  0.120, 7),
        (8192,  0.150, 6),
        (16384, 0.180, 5),
        (32768, 0.000, 4),  # last stage consumes remaining search time
    ]

    for n, frac, n_starts in stages:
        if time.monotonic() > usable_deadline:
            break
        if frac > 0.0:
            stage_time = search_time * float(frac)
        else:
            stage_time = max(0.0, usable_deadline - time.monotonic())
        stage_time = min(stage_time, max(0.0, usable_deadline - time.monotonic()))
        if stage_time < 5.0:
            break

        extra = []
        if pool:
            sorted_pool = sort_scored(pool)
            for i in range(min(3, len(sorted_pool))):
                extra.append(sorted_pool[i][1])

        try:
            results = run_stage(n, stage_time, n_starts,
                                global_best=best_arr if np.isfinite(best_score) else None,
                                prior=prior, extra=extra, rng=rng)
        except Exception:
            results = []

        for sc, arr in results:
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                update_pool(sc, arr, pool, max_items=24)
                if sc < best_score:
                    best_score = sc
                    best_arr = arr.copy()

        print(f"stage length={int(n)} internal_best={best_score:.10f}")

    # Quick length variants around the best known internal sequence.
    if best_arr is not None and best_arr.size > 0:
        for mult in (0.75, 1.25, 1.5):
            n2 = int(best_arr.size * mult)
            if 256 <= n2 <= 32768:
                try:
                    a2 = resample_sequence(best_arr, n2)
                    s2 = quick_score(a2)
                except Exception:
                    s2 = np.inf
                    a2 = None
                if a2 is not None and np.isfinite(s2):
                    update_pool(s2, a2, pool, max_items=24)
                    if s2 < best_score:
                        best_score = s2
                        best_arr = a2.copy()

    if best_arr is None or best_arr.size == 0:
        best_arr = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64) / 1024.0
        best_score = quick_score(best_arr)

    # Final exact verification of a few internal best candidates, if time remains.
    eval_deadline = t0 + max(60.0, total_time - 10.0)
    best_official = np.inf
    official_arr = None

    eval_candidates = [(best_score, best_arr.copy())]
    seen = set()
    seen.add((int(best_arr.size), int(round(float(best_score) * 100000.0))))
    if pool:
        spool = sort_scored(pool)
        for sc, arr in spool:
            if len(eval_candidates) >= 8:
                break
            if arr is None or arr.size == 0:
                continue
            n = int(arr.size)
            if n > 40000:
                continue
            if not np.isfinite(sc):
                continue
            if best_official < np.inf and sc > best_official + 0.02:
                continue
            key = (n, int(round(float(sc) * 100000.0)))
            if key in seen:
                continue
            seen.add(key)
            eval_candidates.append((sc, arr.copy()))

    for sc, arr in eval_candidates:
        if time.monotonic() >= eval_deadline - 8.0:
            break
        if arr is None or arr.size == 0:
            continue
        n = int(arr.size)
        if n > 40000:
            continue
        # Be conservative about exact direct convolution for very long arrays.
        if n > 30000 and (eval_deadline - time.monotonic()) < 90.0:
            continue
        if best_official < np.inf and sc > best_official + 0.02:
            continue

        scale = max(1.0, float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n))))
        out = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64) * scale
        out = np.clip(out, 0.0, None)
        off = exact_evaluate(out)
        print(f"exact length={n} official={off:.10f}")
        if np.isfinite(off) and off < best_official:
            best_official = off
            official_arr = out.copy()

    if official_arr is None:
        n = int(best_arr.size)
        scale = max(1.0, float(np.sqrt(2.0 * float(n))))
        official_arr = np.asarray(best_arr, dtype=np.float64) * scale

    out = np.asarray(official_arr, dtype=np.float64)
    out = np.nan_to_num(out, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, None)

    if out.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(out)) or float(out.sum()) <= 1e-8:
        out = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64) / 1024.0
        out *= float(np.sqrt(2048.0))

    if out.size:
        mv = float(np.max(out))
        if mv > 1000.0:
            out = out * (900.0 / mv)

    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
